   47: \begin{document}
   48: \maketitle
   49: 
   50: \begin{abstract}
   51: For each positive integer $n$, let $B_n$ denote the set of divisors of the
   52: Fibonacci number $F_n$ whose rank of apparition under the map $\alpha$ equals
   53: $n$. We describe $B_n$ as an upper set in the divisor lattice of $F_n$ and
   54: classify its minimal elements by irredundant witness covers of the prime
   55: coordinates of $n$. The resulting support budget $\omega(m)\leq\omega(n)$ is
   56: best possible as a uniform bound. We construct a rank-pure exact-rank-prime
   57: sector that embeds every irredundant cover of the prime coordinates except for
   58: at most two explicitly identified exceptional supports, preserving the complete
   59: support hypergraph. If $k=\omega(n)$ and $R(n)$ is the largest number of
   60: atomic prime powers with one fixed essential/full support pair, then, uniformly
   61: as $k\to\infty$,
   62: \[
   63:  \log\#\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}\geq
   64:  \frac{\log 2}{4}k^2+k\log2-\frac12\log k+O(1),
   65:  \qquad
   66:  \log\#\mathcal M_n
   67:  \leq \frac{\log 2}{4}k^2+k\log R(n)+O(k).
   68: \]
   69: The lower bound has an explicit parity-dependent Jacobi theta constant and is
   70: realized by connected minimal generators. It does not imply that disconnected
   71: generators are negligible in the full arithmetic count. The upper estimate is
   72: not an asymptotic formula without additional control of the unresolved quantity
   73: $R(n)$. We prove that the mean contribution from aggregating several exact-rank
   74: classes in one support window is only $O(x\log\log x)$, reducing the mean
   75: interface to a visible single-rank maximum. We also show that a published
   76: conjecture of Bugeaud--Luca--Mignotte--Siksek would contradict the resulting
   77: mean sparse-window hypothesis, and give a blockwise condition under which
   78: disconnected generators are exponentially negligible. Finally, we classify
   79: the cases $\omega(n)\leq3$ and prove unique connected-block factorizations for
   80: $B_n$ and its minimal elements.
   81: For the remaining single-rank maximum, a fibotomic argument gives the
   82: unconditional pointwise estimate
   83: $a(d)\leq(\log\varphi/2+o(1))\phi_{\rm E}(d)/\log d$, where
   84: $\varphi=(1+\sqrt5)/2$ and $\phi_{\rm E}$ is Euler's function, together with
   85: new unconditional upper and lower bounds for its weighted mean contribution.
   86: \end{abstract}
   87: 
   88: \medskip
   89: \noindent\textbf{Keywords.} Fibonacci numbers, rank of apparition, divisor
   90: lattices, upper sets, witness covers, atomic decompositions.
   91: 
   92: \smallskip
   93: \noindent\textbf{Mathematics Subject Classification (2020).}
   94: 11B39, 11A07, 11A25.
   95: 
   96: %% ====================================================================
   97: \section{Introduction}\label{sec:intro}
   98: %% ====================================================================
   99: 
  100: For an integer $q\ge 1$, the \emph{rank of apparition} (or \emph{order of
  101: appearance}) $\alpha(q)$ is the least positive index $r$ such that $q\mid
  102: F_r$, where $(F_r)_{r\ge 0}$ denotes the Fibonacci sequence with $F_0=0$,
  103: $F_1=1$; the existence of this least index for every $q$ is recorded in
  104: Lemma~\ref{lem:finite-rank}. We use the harmless conventions $\alpha(1)=1$ and
  105: $D_1=\operatorname{Div}(F_1)=\{1\}$ in divisor-lattice formulas. For each
  106: $n\ge 2$, write
  107: \[
  108: D_n:=\operatorname{Div}(F_n),
  109: \qquad
  110: B_n:=\{q\in D_n:\ \alpha(q)=n\},
  111: \qquad
  112: A(n):=\#B_n.
  113: \]
  114: Let $\mathcal P(n)$ be the set of prime divisors of $n$, and let
  115: $\mathcal M_n$ denote the set of minimal elements of $B_n$ under divisibility.
  116: Thus $B_n$ consists of those moduli that first divide a Fibonacci number at
  117: index~$n$. Since $F_2=1$, the boundary layer is empty: throughout the paper
  118: we use the convention $B_2=\mathcal M_2=\varnothing$ and $A(2)=0$.
  119: Throughout, $\log$ is the natural logarithm, $\omega(t)$ is the number of
  120: distinct prime divisors of $t$, and $\Omega(t)$ counts prime divisors with
  121: multiplicity. We write $A_{\rm pr}(n)$ for the number of primes of exact rank
  122: $n$, $\mathsf{Part}(X)$ for the set of set partitions of a finite set $X$,
  123: and $\mathsf B_k$ for the $k$th Bell number. A hypergraph on one vertex is
  124: regarded as connected. These conventions also apply when the notation first
  125: appears in a later section.
  126: The counting function $A(n)$ is subsumed by the
  127: $\alpha$-contraction formalism of Stroi\'nski \cite{Stroinski2016AlphaContraction};
  128: see also the classical M\"obius inversions discussed in
  129: \cite{Halton1966DivisibilityFibonacci,FultonMorris1969ArithmeticalFunctions}.
  130: In this paper we go beyond enumeration and study $B_n$ as an
  131: ordered subset of the divisor lattice $(D_n,\mid)$, asking how it sits inside
  132: $D_n$, how it is generated, and what combinatorial structure it carries.
  133: 
  134: Three classical facts underlie the analysis. First, $q\mid F_m$ if and only if
  135: $\alpha(q)\mid m$
  136: \cite{Wall1960FibonacciModm,Vinson1963RankApparition}. Second, the Fibonacci
  137: sequence satisfies strong divisibility: $\gcd(F_a,F_b)=F_{\gcd(a,b)}$.
  138: Third, ranks of apparition respect least common multiples:
  139: $\alpha(\lcmop(u,v))=\lcmop(\alpha(u),\alpha(v))$; Renault proves the
  140: corresponding identity in the more general $(a,b)$-Fibonacci setting
  141: \cite{Renault2013PeriodRankOrder}. These properties, combined with the
  142: primitive divisor theorem for Fibonacci numbers
  143: \cite{Carmichael1913PrimitiveDivisors,BiluHanrotVoutier2001PrimitiveDivisorsLucas},
  144: are the sole inputs for the main results.
  145: 
  146: This point of view complements several nearby strands in the literature.
  147: Luca and Pomerance \cite{LucaPomerance2014LocalBehavior} study the local
  148: behavior of $\alpha$ under additive shifts; Trojovsk\'a
  149: \cite{Trojovska2020PeriodicPoints} and FitzGibbons--Javaheri--Miller--Verga
  150: \cite{FitzGibbonsJavaheriMillerVerga2025Dynamics} study periodic points and
  151: iteration dynamics of $\alpha$; Byer--Dvorachek--Eckard--Harrington--Wise--Wong
  152: \cite{CameronCountsLundMillerPiechnikWong2022Fibotomic} study fibotomic
  153: polynomials and related algebraic factorizations; and Sanna
  154: \cite{Sanna2016LucasSequences} and Marques \cite{Marques2012OrderAppearance}
  155: study the growth and fixed-point behavior of the order-of-appearance map. The
  156: present paper instead fixes a single fiber $B_n$ and studies its divisibility
  157: geometry inside $D_n$. The upper-fiber identity, the coordinatewise minimal-lcm
  158: criterion, the prime--ladder alternative, the private-coordinate injection, and
  159: the coprimality of disjoint coordinate blocks are recorded below as lemmas:
  160: they are formal consequences of the cited classical identities and are not
  161: claimed as independent arithmetic advances. The contributions developed from
  162: these facts are the witness-cover formulation, the exhaustive support-three
  163: classification with its arithmetic realization test, the passage of connected
  164: block factorization to minimal elements, and the support-entropy coefficient
  165: $\log 2/4$. A further contribution is the support-preserving rank-pure
  166: embedding of arbitrary minimal set covers, which transfers the classical theta
  167: asymptotic and a discrete central local limit to an explicit Fibonacci sector.
  168: These lower results isolate, rather than resolve, the arithmetic multiplicity
  169: $R(n)$. Accordingly, we make no unconditional claim of a sharp asymptotic for
  170: $\#\mathcal M_n$ or of the negligibility of its disconnected part. We do,
  171: however, isolate the mean interface further: support-window aggregation costs
  172: only $O(x\log\log x)$ on average, so only a visible single-rank maximum remains.
  173: A fibotomic size estimate and the congruence restrictions on exact-rank primes
  174: then yield a uniform entropy inequality for each exact-rank class and a
  175: pointwise bound of order $\phi_{\rm E}(d)/\log d$. This improves the available
  176: single-rank input but does not settle the mean sparse-window hypothesis.
  177: A conditional dominance theorem also identifies the stronger blockwise control
  178: that would make the disconnected part exponentially negligible. The mean
  179: sparse-window hypothesis is not presented as a prediction: Conjecture~5.1 of
  180: Bugeaud--Luca--Mignotte--Siksek
  181: \cite{BugeaudLucaMignotteSiksek2005FewPrimeDivisors} would imply its failure.
  182: 
  183: Call a prime power $\theta=p^e$ \emph{atomic for the index $d$} if
  184: $\alpha(\theta)=d$ and $\alpha(p^{e-1})<d$. An \emph{$n$-witness cover} is a
  185: pairwise coprime set of such atomic prime powers whose apparition indices
  186: collectively span the prime factorization of $n$ in a minimal, irredundant
  187: fashion; the full definition is Definition~\ref{def:witness-cover}.
  188: 
  189: \begin{previewtheorem}[Witness-cover theorem]
  190: For every $n\ge2$, multiplication gives a bijection from $n$-witness
  191: covers $\Theta$ to minimal generators:
  192: \[
  193: \mathcal M_n=
  194: \left\{\prod_{\theta\in\Theta}\theta:\ \Theta\text{ is an $n$-witness cover}
  195: \right\}.
  196: \]
  197: Consequently every $m\in\mathcal M_n$ satisfies
  198: $\omega(m)\le\omega(n)$. This support budget is globally sharp in the precise
  199: sense that no smaller bound depending only on $\omega(n)$ is valid; equality
  200: is constructed on every odd layer $n\ge3$. The precise cover conditions are
  201: given in Definition~\ref{def:witness-cover}, and the result is
  202: Theorem~\ref{thm:witness-classification} together with
  203: Corollaries~\ref{cor:support-bound},~\ref{cor:support-bound-sharp},
  204: and~\ref{cor:support-budget-best-possible}.
  205: \end{previewtheorem}
  206: 
  207: The witness classification also controls the number of minimal generators,
  208: not merely their form.
  209: 
  210: \begin{previewtheorem}[Support-entropy theorem]
  211: For $n\ge3$, let $k=\omega(n)$ and let $R(n)$ be the largest atomic-family
  212: multiplicity at one essential/full support pair. If $k\ge3$ and
  213: $r=\lfloor k/2\rfloor$, then
  214: \[
  215:  (2^r-1)^{k-r}\le\#\mathcal M_n
  216:  \le\sum_{s=1}^{k}\binom{k}{s}(2R(n))^s2^{s(k-s)}.
  217: \]
  218: The rank-pure sector gives the stronger connected lower bound
  219: \[
  220:  \log\#\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}\ge
  221:  \frac{\log2}{4}k^2+k\log2-\frac12\log k+O(1)
  222: \]
  223: uniformly as $k\to\infty$, with an explicit parity-dependent theta constant.
  224: The coefficient $\log2/4$ is exact for the support combinatorics. These lower
  225: bounds do not give an asymptotic formula for $\log\#\mathcal M_n$ unless
  226: $R(n)=\exp(o(\omega(n)))$. Separate conjectures about $R(n)$ imply conditional
  227: normal-order and mean equivalents. These statements are
  228: Theorems~\ref{thm:rank-pure-universality} and
  229: \ref{thm:minimal-generator-growth}, together with their corollaries.
  230: \end{previewtheorem}
  231: 
  232: Making this classification effective requires the prime-power apparition
  233: formulas of Fulton--Morris \cite{FultonMorris1969ArithmeticalFunctions} and
  234: Lengyel \cite{Lengyel1995OrderFibonacciLucas}. These show that every atomic
  235: factor is either a \emph{prime atom} or lies on a
  236: \emph{prime-power ladder} (changing exactly one prime coordinate of the
  237: apparition index). This dichotomy
  238: (Lemma~\ref{thm:primitive-ladder-dichotomy}) enables complete
  239: classifications of $\mathcal M_n$ when $\omega(n)\le 3$. The detailed
  240: ingredients are Corollary~\ref{cor:two-support-complete-classification} and
  241: Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-nonemptiness}; the consolidated
  242: classification is Theorem~\ref{thm:low-support-classification-package}. The
  243: corresponding complete description of the full birth layer $B_n$ is obtained by
  244: taking the upper ideals generated by these listed minimal elements; this is
  245: recorded in Corollary~\ref{cor:low-support-birth-layer-package}.
  246: 
  247: The same lcm mechanism gives a local-to-global factorization over connected
  248: coordinate blocks.
  249: 
  250: \begin{previewtheorem}[Connected block factorization]
  251: Let $q\in B_n$ and form the support hypergraph on $\mathcal P(n)$ whose
  252: edges are the prime-coordinate supports of the ranks of the prime-power
  253: factors of $q$. If its connected components are $C$, then
  254: \[
  255: q=\prod_C q_C,\qquad q_C\in B_{n_C},
  256: \qquad n_C=\prod_{\ell\in C}\ell^{\nu_\ell(n)} ,
  257: \]
  258: and this factorization is unique. The same statement holds for minimal
  259: generators after replacing $B_{n_C}$ by $\mathcal M_{n_C}$. Consequently
  260: $A(n)$ and $\#\mathcal M_n$ are partition sums over the connected counts.
  261: This is proved in Section~\ref{sec:connected}.
  262: \end{previewtheorem}
  263: 
  264: As secondary consequences, nonexceptional birth layers contain principal
  265: intervals natural after choosing an exact-rank prime
  266: (Corollary~\ref{cor:canonical-principal-interval}), Boolean subposets of rank
  267: $\tau(n)-O(1)$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:boolean-skeleton}), and---for odd
  268: $n$---realizations of every minimal cover of $\mathcal P(n)$
  269: (Corollary~\ref{cor:odd-rank-pure-universality}).
  270: 
  271: The paper is organized as follows. Section~\ref{sec:prelim} collects classical
  272: background on Fibonacci divisibility and exact-rank primes.
  273: Section~\ref{sec:upper-fibers} first records the general fiber and witness-cover
  274: structure. Section~\ref{sec:low-support} then introduces the Fibonacci-specific
  275: prime-power arithmetic and the low-support classifications.
  276: Section~\ref{sec:connected} proves the connected-block factorization
  277: (Theorems~\ref{thm:birth-connected-factorization}
  278: and~\ref{thm:mingenerator-connected-factorization}) and the partition-lattice
  279: formulas.
  280: Section~\ref{sec:growth} proves rank-pure universality, the theta-refined
  281: support-entropy bounds, and the discrete local limit, before isolating the
  282: unresolved arithmetic interface. Section~\ref{sec:numerical} presents the
  283: finite verification, and
  284: Section~\ref{sec:questions} records further questions.
  285: 
  286: %% ====================================================================
  287: \section{Preliminaries}\label{sec:prelim}
  288: %% ====================================================================
  289: 
  290: \subsection{Rank of apparition and Fibonacci divisibility}\label{subsec:fib-div}
  291: 
  292: \begin{lemma}[Finite rank of apparition]\label{lem:finite-rank}
  293: For every integer $q\ge1$, the rank of apparition $\alpha(q)$ is finite.  More
  294: precisely, $\alpha(1)=1$, and for every $q\ge2$ there is a positive integer
  295: $r$ such that $q\mid F_r$.
  296: \end{lemma}
  297: 
  298: \begin{proof}
  299: The case $q=1$ is the stated convention.  Let $q\ge2$.  Work modulo $q$ with
  300: the Fibonacci state vector $(F_m,F_{m+1})$.  The transition
  301: \[

 succeeded in 333ms:
 2231: \#\mathcal M_n\ge \mathsf B_{\omega(n)},
 2232: \]
 2233: and, since the minimal generators form an antichain in the divisor lattice,
 2234: \[
 2235: \widthop(B_n)\ge \#\mathcal M_n\ge \mathsf B_{\omega(n)},
 2236: \]
 2237: where $\mathsf B_k$ is the $k$th Bell number.
 2238: \end{corollary}
 2239: 
 2240: \begin{proof}
 2241: Since $n$ is odd and $n\ge3$, every nonempty $n_S$ is odd and at least $3$;
 2242: therefore $n_S\notin\{6,12\}$, and Theorem~\ref{thm:primitive-divisors}
 2243: supplies the required exact-rank prime $\pi_S$. For each block $C$, the prime
 2244: $\pi_C$ is a prime atom of rank $n_C$ and
 2245: therefore belongs to $\mathcal M_{n_C}^{\mathrm{conn}}$: its support
 2246: hypergraph has the single edge $C$. Distinct blocks have coprime Fibonacci
 2247: ambient factors by Lemma~\ref{thm:coprime-embedding}, so the primes
 2248: $\pi_C$ are pairwise coprime. Theorem~\ref{thm:mingenerator-connected-factorization}
 2249: then gives $m_\Pi\in\mathcal M_n$.
 2250: 
 2251: The connected-component partition of $m_\Pi$ is exactly $\Pi$, so different
 2252: set partitions give different minimal generators. There are
 2253: $\mathsf B_{\omega(n)}$ set partitions of $\mathcal P(n)$.
 2254: Finally, no two distinct elements of $\mathcal M_n$ are comparable under
 2255: divisibility: if $m,m'\in\mathcal M_n$ and $m\mid m'$ with $m\ne m'$, then
 2256: $m'$ is not minimal in the upper set $B_n$. Hence $\mathcal M_n$ is an
 2257: antichain in $B_n$, so $\widthop(B_n)\ge\#\mathcal M_n$.
 2258: \end{proof}

 2260: %% ====================================================================
 2261: \section{Support entropy and the unresolved arithmetic multiplicity}
 2262: \label{sec:growth}
 2263: %% ====================================================================
 2264: 
 2265: Write $\Omega(N)=\sum_p\nu_p(N)$ for the number of prime factors of $N$
 2266: counted with multiplicity. Set $R(2)=1$, consistently with
 2267: $\mathcal M_2=\varnothing$. For $n\ge3$ define the maximal atomic rank-window
 2268: multiplicity
 2269: \begin{equation}\label{eq:rank-window-multiplicity}
 2270:  R(n):=\max\!\left\{1,
 2271:  \max_{\varnothing\ne I\subseteq J\subseteq\mathcal P(n)}
 2272:        \#\mathcal A_{I,J}(n)\right\}.
 2273: \end{equation}
 2274: By the prime--ladder alternative, the nonsingleton families counted here are the
 2275: exact-rank windows $P_J(n)$, whereas every ladder family contributes at most
 2276: one atom. Thus $R(n)$ records exactly the arithmetic multiplicity not fixed by
 2277: the support combinatorics.
 2278: 
 2279: We first remove the contribution caused solely by collecting several exact
 2280: rank classes in one support window. For $d\mid n$, write
 2281: \[
 2282:  T_n(d):=\{\ell\in\mathcal P(n):
 2283:  \nu_\ell(d)=\nu_\ell(n)\}.
 2284: \]
 2285: 
 2286: \begin{theorem}[Mean rank-window deaggregation]
 2287: \label{thm:mean-rank-window-deaggregation}
 2288: Put
 2289: \[
 2290:  a(d):=\#\Pi_\alpha(d),\qquad
 2291:  \mathcal D^+(n):=\{d\mid n:T_n(d)\ne\varnothing\},
 2292: \]
 2293: and
 2294: \[
 2295:  A^*(n):=\max\left\{1,\max_{d\in\mathcal D^+(n)}a(d)\right\}.
 2296: \]
 2297: Then, as $x\to\infty$,
 2298: \begin{equation}\label{eq:mean-window-deaggregation}
 2299:  \sum_{3\le n\le x}\omega(n)\log R(n)
 2300:  =\sum_{3\le n\le x}\omega(n)\log A^*(n)
 2301:  +O(x\log\log x).
 2302: \end{equation}
 2303: Consequently the mean sparse-window hypothesis \textup{(H2)} is equivalent to
 2304: \begin{equation}\label{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}
 2305:  \sum_{3\le n\le x}\omega(n)
 2306:  \log\max\left\{1,
 2307:  \max_{\substack{d\mid n\\T_n(d)\ne\varnothing}}
 2308:  \#\Pi_\alpha(d)\right\}
 2309:  =o\bigl(x(\log\log x)^2\bigr).
 2310: \end{equation}
 2311: \end{theorem}
 2312: 
 2313: \begin{proof}
 2314: For nonempty $J\subseteq\mathcal P(n)$, let
 2315: \[
 2316:  W_J(n):=\{d\mid n:T_n(d)=J\}.
 2317: \]
 2318: If $n=\prod_\ell\ell^{e_\ell}$, then
 2319: \begin{equation}\label{eq:support-window-size}
 2320:  \#W_J(n)=\prod_{\ell\notin J}e_\ell.
 2321: \end{equation}
 2322: The prime atoms in the family with essential and full supports $(J,J)$ form
 2323: the disjoint union
 2324: \[
 2325:  \coprod_{d\in W_J(n)}\Pi_\alpha(d),
 2326: \]
 2327: because a prime has a unique rank of apparition. By
 2328: Lemma~\ref{thm:primitive-ladder-dichotomy}, every remaining effective atomic
 2329: family is a ladder family with singleton essential support. Once that
 2330: essential coordinate $\ell$ is fixed, the underlying prime is $\ell$, and
 2331: the full-exponent equation in Proposition~\ref{prop:prime-power-ladders}
 2332: determines at most one ladder exponent. Therefore, with
 2333: \[
 2334:  P(n):=\max_{\varnothing\ne J\subseteq\mathcal P(n)}
 2335:        \sum_{d\in W_J(n)}a(d),
 2336: \]
 2337: one has
 2338: \begin{equation}\label{eq:prime-window-versus-R}
 2339:  P(n)\le R(n)\le P(n)+1.
 2340: \end{equation}
 2341: Every $d\in\mathcal D^+(n)$ belongs to one of the windows above. Hence
 2342: \[
 2343:  A^*(n)\le R(n)
 2344:  \le1+A^*(n)\max_J\#W_J(n)
 2345:  \le1+A^*(n)\prod_{\ell^{e_\ell}\parallel n}e_\ell.
 2346: \]
 2347: Writing
 2348: \[
 2349:  h(n):=\sum_{\ell^{e_\ell}\parallel n}\log e_\ell,
 2350: \]
 2351: and using $A^*(n)\ge1$ gives
 2352: \begin{equation}\label{eq:pointwise-window-deaggregation}
 2353:  0\le\log R(n)-\log A^*(n)\le\log2+h(n).
 2354: \end{equation}
 2355: For each exponent $e\ge2$, $\log e\le\sum_{2\le j\le e}\log j$, so
 2356: \[
 2357:  h(n)\le\sum_{\substack{p^j\mid n\\j\ge2}}\log j.
 2358: \]
 2359: The classical mean estimate \eqref{eq:mean-omega-input}, used uniformly with
 2360: $\log\log(3y)$ in place of $\log\log y$ for small $y$, yields
 2361: \begin{align*}
 2362:  \sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)h(n)
 2363:  &\le\sum_{\substack{p^j\le x\\j\ge2}}\log j
 2364:        \sum_{m\le x/p^j}\omega(p^jm)\\
 2365:  &\le\sum_{\substack{p^j\le x\\j\ge2}}\log j
 2366:        \sum_{m\le x/p^j}(1+\omega(m))\\
 2367:  &\ll x\log\log x\sum_p\sum_{j\ge2}\frac{\log j}{p^j}
 2368:  \ll x\log\log x.
 2369: \end{align*}
 2370: The double series converges absolutely. Summing
 2371: \eqref{eq:pointwise-window-deaggregation} and using
 2372: $\sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)\ll x\log\log x$ proves
 2373: \eqref{eq:mean-window-deaggregation}; the stated equivalence follows because
 2374: $x\log\log x=o(x(\log\log x)^2)$.
 2375: \end{proof}
 2376: 
 2377: \begin{corollary}[Single-rank sufficient and necessary interfaces]
 2378: \label{cor:single-rank-mean-interfaces}
 2379: A sufficient condition for \textup{(H2)} is
 2380: \begin{equation}\label{eq:single-rank-sufficient-condition}
 2381:  \sum_{d\le x}
 2382:  \frac{(\omega(d)+\log\log x)\log\max\{1,a(d)\}}{d}
 2383:  =o\bigl((\log\log x)^2\bigr).
 2384: \end{equation}
 2385: Conversely, \textup{(H2)} implies the diagonal necessary condition
 2386: \begin{equation}\label{eq:single-rank-necessary-condition}
 2387:  \sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)\log\max\{1,a(n)\}
 2388:  =o\bigl(x(\log\log x)^2\bigr).
 2389: \end{equation}
 2390: \end{corollary}
 2391: 
 2392: \begin{proof}
 2393: Since
 2394: \[
 2395:  \log A^*(n)\le\sum_{d\mid n}\log\max\{1,a(d)\},
 2396: \]
 2397: the substitution $n=dm$ and $\omega(dm)\le\omega(d)+\omega(m)$ gives
 2398: \[
 2399:  \sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)\log A^*(n)
 2400:  \ll x\sum_{d\le x}
 2401:  \frac{(\omega(d)+\log\log x)\log\max\{1,a(d)\}}{d}.
 2402: \]
 2403: Thus \eqref{eq:single-rank-sufficient-condition} and
 2404: Theorem~\ref{thm:mean-rank-window-deaggregation} imply \textup{(H2)}. For the
 2405: necessary condition, $n\in\mathcal D^+(n)$, and hence
 2406: $R(n)\ge A^*(n)\ge\max\{1,a(n)\}$; summation proves the claim.
 2407: \end{proof}
 2408: 
 2409: \begin{theorem}[Fibotomic rank entropy]
 2410: \label{thm:fibotomic-rank-entropy}
 2411: Let $\varphi=(1+\sqrt5)/2$, let $\phi_{\rm E}$ denote Euler's totient
 2412: function, and, for $d\geq3$, put
 2413: \[
 2414:  \mathfrak F_d:=\prod_{r\mid d}F_r^{\mu(d/r)}.
 2415: \]
 2416: For $k\geq0$, set $m=\lfloor k/2\rfloor$ and
 2417: \[
 2418:  \mathcal H_d(k):=
 2419:  k\log\frac{2d}{3}+2\log(m!)+(k-2m)\log(m+1).
 2420: \]
 2421: There is an absolute constant $C_F$ such that, for every $d\geq3$,
 2422: \begin{equation}\label{eq:fibotomic-rank-entropy}
 2423:  \mathcal H_d(a(d))\leq \phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi+C_F.
 2424: \end{equation}
 2425: More generally, every finitely supported family of nonnegative real numbers
 2426: $(\lambda_d)_{d\geq3}$ satisfies
 2427: \begin{equation}\label{eq:weighted-fibotomic-rank-entropy}
 2428:  \sum_{d\geq3}\lambda_d\mathcal H_d(a(d))
 2429:  \leq (\log\varphi)\sum_{d\geq3}\lambda_d\phi_{\rm E}(d)
 2430:        +C_F\sum_{d\geq3}\lambda_d.
 2431: \end{equation}
 2432: Uniformly as $d\to\infty$,
 2433: \begin{equation}\label{eq:pointwise-exact-rank-bound}
 2434:  a(d)\leq
 2435:  \left(\frac{\log\varphi}{2}+o(1)\right)
 2436:  \frac{\phi_{\rm E}(d)}{\log d}.
 2437: \end{equation}
 2438: Consequently, uniformly as $n\to\infty$,
 2439: \begin{equation}\label{eq:visible-exact-rank-bound}
 2440:  A^*(n)\leq
 2441:  \left(\frac{\log\varphi}{2}+o(1)\right)
 2442:  \frac{\phi_{\rm E}(n)}{\log n}.
 2443: \end{equation}
 2444: \end{theorem}
 2445: 
 2446: \begin{proof}
 2447: Let $\Psi_d(x)$ be the $d$th fibotomic polynomial. The fibotomic
 2448: factorization of Byer--Dvorachek--Eckard--Harrington--Wise--Wong
 2449: \cite{CameronCountsLundMillerPiechnikWong2022Fibotomic} gives
 2450: \[
 2451:  F_d(x)=\prod_{r\mid d}\Psi_r(x),
 2452:  \qquad
 2453:  \mathfrak F_d=\Psi_d(1)\in\mathbb N.
 2454: \]
 2455: This published factorization is used as an input and is not asserted as a
 2456: result of the present paper.
 2457: Binet's formula, in the form
 2458: \[
 2459:  F_r=\frac{\varphi^r}{\sqrt5}
 2460:  \bigl(1-(-\varphi^{-2})^r\bigr),
 2461: \]
 2462: and the identities
 2463: $\sum_{r\mid d}\mu(d/r)=0$ and
 2464: $\sum_{r\mid d}r\mu(d/r)=\phi_{\rm E}(d)$ show that
 2465: \begin{equation}\label{eq:fibotomic-binet-error}
 2466:  \log\mathfrak F_d=\phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi+E_d,
 2467:  \qquad
 2468:  |E_d|\leq
 2469:  C_F:=\sum_{j\geq1}
 2470:  \left|\log\bigl(1-(-\varphi^{-2})^j\bigr)\right|.
 2471: \end{equation}
 2472: The series defining $C_F$ converges geometrically.
 2473: 
 2474: If $p\in\Pi_\alpha(d)$, then $p\mid F_d$ but $p\nmid F_r$ for every
 2475: proper divisor $r$ of $d$. Since
 2476: $F_d=\prod_{r\mid d}\mathfrak F_r$ and each factor is an integer, $p$ must
 2477: divide $\mathfrak F_d$. Distinct exact-rank primes are coprime, and hence
 2478: \begin{equation}\label{eq:exact-rank-radical-divides-fibotomic}
 2479:  \prod_{p\in\Pi_\alpha(d)}p\mid\mathfrak F_d.
 2480: \end{equation}
 2481: For $p\notin\{2,5\}$, the classical rank congruence gives
 2482: \[
 2483:  d=\alpha(p)\mid p-\left(\frac5p\right),
 2484: \]
 2485: see \cite{Wall1960FibonacciModm,Vinson1963RankApparition}. Thus
 2486: $p\equiv\pm1\pmod d$. The exceptional ranks $d=3,5$ have respectively
 2487: the single exact-rank primes $2,5$ and satisfy the estimate directly. For
 2488: every other $d$, write the exact-rank primes in increasing order as
 2489: $p_1<\cdots<p_k$. There are at most two candidates in each pair of residue
 2490: classes $jd\pm1$, whence
 2491: \[
 2492:  p_i\geq d\left\lceil\frac i2\right\rceil-1
 2493:  \geq\frac{2d}{3}\left\lceil\frac i2\right\rceil.
 2494: \]
 2495: It follows that, with $m=\lfloor k/2\rfloor$,
 2496: \begin{equation}\label{eq:rank-congruence-product-bound}
 2497:  \prod_{i=1}^kp_i\geq
 2498:  \left(\frac{2d}{3}\right)^k(m!)^2(m+1)^{k-2m}.
 2499: \end{equation}
 2500: Combining \eqref{eq:fibotomic-binet-error}--
 2501: \eqref{eq:rank-congruence-product-bound} and taking logarithms proves
 2502: \eqref{eq:fibotomic-rank-entropy}. Multiplication by $\lambda_d\geq0$ and
 2503: finite summation proves \eqref{eq:weighted-fibotomic-rank-entropy}.
 2504: 
 2505: Stirling's lower bound applied in \eqref{eq:fibotomic-rank-entropy} gives,
 2506: with an absolute implied constant,
 2507: \begin{equation}\label{eq:rank-entropy-coarse}
 2508:  a(d)\bigl(\log d+\log\max\{1,a(d)\}-O(1)\bigr)
 2509:  \leq \phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi+O(1).
 2510: \end{equation}
 2511: If $a(d)\leq\phi_{\rm E}(d)/(\log d)^2$, then
 2512: \eqref{eq:pointwise-exact-rank-bound} follows immediately. Otherwise the
 2513: standard uniform lower bound
 2514: $\phi_{\rm E}(d)\gg d/\log\log(3d)$
 2515: \cite{HardyWright2008Introduction} implies
 2516: \[
 2517:  \log a(d)=\log d+O(\log\log d)=\log d+o(\log d).
 2518: \]
 2519: Substitution in \eqref{eq:rank-entropy-coarse} yields
 2520: $(2+o(1))a(d)\log d\leq\phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi$, proving
 2521: \eqref{eq:pointwise-exact-rank-bound} uniformly.
 2522: 
 2523: For \eqref{eq:visible-exact-rank-bound}, the case $A^*(n)=1$ follows from
 2524: $\phi_{\rm E}(n)/\log n\to\infty$. Otherwise choose
 2525: $d_n\in\mathcal D^+(n)$ with $A^*(n)=a(d_n)$. The preceding estimate has
 2526: the coarse consequence $a(d)\ll d/\log(2d)$. If
 2527: $d_n\leq n/(\log n)^3$, the lower bound for $\phi_{\rm E}(n)$ gives
 2528: \[
 2529:  a(d_n)=o\left(\frac{\phi_{\rm E}(n)}{\log n}\right).
 2530: \]
 2531: If $d_n>n/(\log n)^3$, then
 2532: $\log d_n=(1+o(1))\log n$ and, since $d_n\mid n$,
 2533: $\phi_{\rm E}(d_n)\leq\phi_{\rm E}(n)$. Applying
 2534: \eqref{eq:pointwise-exact-rank-bound} at $d_n$ completes the proof.
 2535: \end{proof}
 2536: 
 2537: \begin{corollary}[Bounds for the visible single-rank contribution]
 2538: \label{cor:visible-rank-sum-bounds}
 2539: Put
 2540: \[
 2541:  S(x):=\sum_{3\leq n\leq x}\omega(n)\log A^*(n).
 2542: \]
 2543: Then
 2544: \begin{equation}\label{eq:visible-rank-sum-upper}
 2545:  S(x)\leq
 2546:  \sum_{3\leq n\leq x}\omega(n)\log\phi_{\rm E}(n)
 2547:  -x(\log\log x)^2+O(x\log\log x),
 2548: \end{equation}
 2549: whereas
 2550: \begin{equation}\label{eq:visible-rank-sum-lower}
 2551:  S(x)\geq
 2552:  \left(\frac{2\log2}{25}+o(1)\right)x\log\log x.
 2553: \end{equation}
 2554: Neither estimate settles \eqref{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}.
 2555: \end{corollary}
 2556: 
 2557: \begin{proof}
 2558: Taking logarithms in \eqref{eq:visible-exact-rank-bound}, uniformly for
 2559: large $n$, gives
 2560: \[
 2561:  \log A^*(n)\leq
 2562:  \log\phi_{\rm E}(n)-\log\log n
 2563:  +\log\frac{\log\varphi}{2}+o(1).
 2564: \]
 2565: In addition to \eqref{eq:mean-omega-input}, partial summation gives
 2566: \[
 2567:  \sum_{3\leq n\leq x}\omega(n)\log\log n
 2568:  =x(\log\log x)^2+O(x\log\log x).
 2569: \]
 2570: For example, on $\sqrt x<n\leq x$ one may replace $\log\log n$ by
 2571: $\log\log x$ with $O(1)$ error, while the complementary range contributes
 2572: $o(x)$. Summation proves \eqref{eq:visible-rank-sum-upper}.
 2573: 
 2574: For the lower bound, Jarden proved that $L_{5p}$ has at least two distinct
 2575: primitive prime divisors for every prime $p>5$
 2576: \cite{Jarden1968LucasTwoPrimitive}; this published theorem is used as an
 2577: input and is not asserted as a result of the present paper. If $q$ is one of
 2578: them, then
 2579: $q\mid L_{5p}$ and
 2580: \[
 2581:  F_{10p}=F_{5p}L_{5p},\qquad \gcd(F_{5p},L_{5p})\mid2.
 2582: \]
 2583: Here $q$ is odd, so the gcd identity gives $q\nmid F_{5p}$. Thus
 2584: $\alpha(q)=2r$ for some $r\mid5p$. If $r<5p$, then
 2585: $F_{2r}=F_rL_r$ and $q\nmid F_r$, so $q\mid L_r$, contrary to the
 2586: primitivity of $q$ for $L_{5p}$.
 2587: Consequently $\alpha(q)=10p$, and therefore
 2588: \begin{equation}\label{eq:jarden-exact-rank-consequence}
 2589:  a(10p)\geq2\qquad(p>5\ \hbox{prime}).
 2590: \end{equation}
 2591: 
 2592: Let $n=10m$, where $5\nmid m$ and $m$ has a prime factor $p>5$. Then
 2593: $d=10p$ divides $n$ and
 2594: $\nu_5(d)=\nu_5(n)=1$, so $T_n(d)\ne\varnothing$. By
 2595: \eqref{eq:jarden-exact-rank-consequence}, $A^*(n)\geq2$. The excluded
 2596: integers $m$ having no prime factor greater than $5$ are of the form
 2597: $2^a3^b$ and contribute only $O((\log x)^3)$ after weighting by $\omega$.
 2598: Writing $y=x/10$ and interchanging two finite sums gives
 2599: \[
 2600:  \sum_{\substack{m\leq y\\5\nmid m}}\omega(m)
 2601:  =\sum_{\substack{q\leq y\\q\ \mathrm{prime},\ q\ne5}}
 2602:  \left(\left\lfloor\frac yq\right\rfloor
 2603:  -\left\lfloor\frac y{5q}\right\rfloor\right)
 2604:  =\frac45y\log\log y+O(y).
 2605: \]
 2606: Since $\omega(10m)=\omega(m)+O(1)$, the total $\omega$-weight of the
 2607: constructed integers is
 2608: $(2/25)x\log\log x+O(x)$. Multiplication by $\log2$ proves
 2609: \eqref{eq:visible-rank-sum-lower}.
 2610: \end{proof}

 2613: For a finite set $V$, let $\operatorname{MinCov}(V)$ denote the set of
 2614: minimal, or irredundant, covers of $V$ by nonempty subsets:
 2615: \[
 2616:  \operatorname{MinCov}(V):=
 2617:  \left\{\mathcal C\subseteq 2^V\setminus\{\varnothing\}:
 2618:  \bigcup_{S\in\mathcal C}S=V,\quad
 2619:  S\nsubseteq\bigcup_{T\in\mathcal C\setminus\{S\}}T
 2620:  \ \text{for every }S\in\mathcal C\right\}.
 2621: \]
